compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:632:# Plan: v5 Ã¢â‚¬â€ SageMaker-native Re-implementation of Runnable Claude Code
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:644:The 5-investigator deep dive (Team A1 prompt-engineering, A2 tool-design, B1 session forensics, B2 Hermes/Runnable comparative, Codex deep architectural) identified the root cause as **aggregate prompt growth from multi-source integration** (Hermes patterns + Runnable patterns + Learning_Factory patterns + v4-original additions = ~5000+ static tokens). Each individual integration was reviewed; the aggregate failed.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:646:Round 3 fixes (8 in-place changes) addressed the immediate failure but **could not address the structural cause** without an architectural refactor. Specifically: v4's monolithic 11K-line `sagemaker_agent.py` cannot adopt Runnable's deferred-tool-schema pattern (saves ~5500 tokens/turn) without significant restructuring.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:650:**v5 = SageMaker-native Python re-implementation of Runnable Claude Code, adopting Runnable as the primary textbook, with Hermes and Learning_Factory as support references.**
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:652:- Runnable Claude Code (TypeScript at `D:/Github/sagemaker-coding-agent/_archive/compare_code/gg-claude-code-runnable/`) is the primary architectural source.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:656:**Source-citation rule**: every non-trivial architectural decision must cite a primary source. Runnable is the default. Hermes / Learning_Factory / v4 reuse is allowed only with (a) explicit rationale and (b) a Runnable-non-applicability note in the ADR.

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW.md:3013:These gaps are **fixable in Phase 5-8** and each fix makes v5 > Runnable for correctness.

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:575:.\compact_v4\docs\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT.html:105:  Why is <strong>Hermes</strong> (self-improving agent) different from <strong>Runnable / SageMaker V4</strong> (coding agent)?<br>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:579:.\compact_v4\docs\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT.html:245:    <strong>Key insight:</strong> Runnable and V4 are <em>task runners</em>. They do one job well, then stop. Hermes is a <em>persistent companion</em> that accumulates knowledge. Oracle needs to become the Hermes equivalent for sales Ã¢â‚¬â€ learning from each call and improving its next decision.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:580:.\compact_v4\docs\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT.html:259:  <p class="desc">Left = what Runnable and SageMaker V4 do every session. Right = what Hermes does on top of that. The core loop is identical Ã¢â‚¬â€ the difference is what happens AFTER the response.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:600:.\compact_v4\CHANGELOG.md:203:- **Post-compact restore protocol** Ã¢â‚¬â€ `# System` section of `SYSTEM_PROMPT` now explicitly tells the agent: after auto-compact, do NOT ask the user what to do; read the `[CONVERSATION SUMMARY]` block, the restored TODOs, the recently-read files block, `AGENT_STATUS.md`, and continue from the first unchecked task. The summary's "Pending Questions" section is the FIRST thing to look at on resume. Closes the most common post-compact UX failure: agent gets compacted, then asks "what would you like me to do?" instead of resuming.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:686:.\compact_v4\docs\V4_10_0_PLAN.md:186:**For self-use SageMaker coding agent: v4.10.0 Ã¢â€°Â¥ Runnable on every dimension that matters.** The remaining Runnable advantages depend on infrastructure that doesn't exist on Bedrock/SageMaker (multi-scope cache, ant-only feature gates, GitHub tooling, cron/notifications, 1M beta header). v4.10.0 adds 11 SageMaker-specific advantages Runnable doesn't have (single-file deploy, Bedrock-native auth, AGENT_STATUS.md handoff, custom compaction model, skill self-patching, spec-first critique, mandatory verification, document tools, python_exec, no-network guardrails, dual-gate skill-auto-load default OFF).

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:948:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\v3_architecture.html:215:    <p class="subtitle">A secure AI coding assistant that runs inside AWS SageMaker notebooks. It reads your code, writes files, edits Jupyter notebooks surgically, creates documents, generates charts, and helps you build software &mdash; all through a chat interface. v4.10.0 shipped 5 Runnable-parity upgrades. v4.10.1 added Context Collapse + Sonnet 4.5 default. v4.10.2 softened the verify-contract. v4.10.3 added ship-gate verifier + cache-boundary regression test + many-skill stress test + clearer permission denials. v4.10.4 closed the largest remaining gap: sub-agents now receive a bounded work-context handoff block (AGENT_STATUS slice + active todos + recent changed files) on top of v4.10.0's env-details. v4.10.5 adopted 3 Learning_Factory prompt-only patterns: post-compact resume protocol, structured summary sections, skill self-patching 4-rule check. v4.10.6 added the html skill: presentation / design / flowchart / architecture HTML deliverables, with screenshot-iteration loop. v4.10.7 hardened destructive-command coverage: ~50 new bash patterns + ~12 python patterns spanning cloud / IaC / orchestration / storage / DB CLI / persistence / system-overwrite, HIGH_RISK_TOOLS excluded from Always-Approve UI, cross-surface mirrored into Claude Code + Learning_Factory hooks. v4.10.8 closed the residual obfuscation escape route (base64/xxd/eval pipe-to-shell now blocked across more interpreters) and hard-blocked recursive folder removal from the agent (`rm -r/-rf/-fr/-R/--recursive`, `rmdir`, `Remove-Item -Recurse`, `shutil.rmtree`, `os.rmdir`, `os.removedirs`, `Path(...).rmdir()`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ single-file cleanup still allowed; the Ã°Å¸Â§Â¹ Clean button still works in-process. v4.10.9 closed Codex's third local-hook finding by adding a narrow backtick eval+downloader pattern (defense-in-depth only Ã¢â‚¬â€ eval was already excluded from the bash allowlist; zero new false-positive risk). v4.10.10 surfaced the previously-hardcoded `aws_bedrock_only` setting as a UI checkbox in cell 2 of `chat.ipynb` (default ticked = strict). Codex review on v4.10.10: PASS. 134/134 destructive-coverage tests + 122 v4 unit tests + 16/16 hook obfuscation self-test all green.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:950:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\v3_architecture.html:283:                <div class="card-desc">User asked: <em>"when bedrock only, then just it only Ã¢â‚¬â€ where to set it, in UI to control?"</em>. The previously-hardcoded <code>aws_bedrock_only</code> setting is now surfaced as a checkbox in cell 2 of <code>chat.ipynb</code>. <strong>Default ticked (strict, Bedrock-only).</strong> Untick before running cell 3 to allow S3 read/write, Textract, Lambda invoke, DynamoDB writes Ã¢â‚¬â€ all gated by the approval prompt; destructive AWS operations (delete bucket / object / table / function, terminate instances, etc.) remain hard-blocked at the per-method denylist and admin services blocked at boto3.client level. Cell 3 banner now displays AWS scope at startup. In-place refinements after comprehensive Codex re-review: inline <code>(?i)</code> flag on PowerShell <code>Remove-Item</code> regex (belt-and-suspenders Ã¢â‚¬â€ <code>re.IGNORECASE</code> already applied at matching layer line 1884) + clearer cell-by-cell setup text in cell 0. <strong>Codex re-review: PASS</strong> ("no findings, Remove-Item bypass closed, cell 0 setup text unambiguous, no additional bugs"). 134 destructive-coverage + 122 unit tests still green.</div>

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1129:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_RUNNABLE_VS_LANGGRAPH.html:826:<b>What is enforcement?</b> Enforcement = stopping the AI from doing something dangerous. This is NOT the same as asking the AI nicely ("please don't delete files"). Enforcement means the system STRUCTURALLY PREVENTS the action. The AI can want to <code>rm -rf /</code> all it wants Ã¢â‚¬â€ the enforcement layer makes it impossible. This is the most important lesson from Claude Code.

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1208:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:741:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Runnable targets Anthropic's direct API. Bedrock has different validation rules, pricing, and caching behavior. V4 handles these differences natively so you don't hit surprise errors or pay more than necessary.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1212:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:759:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Runnable outputs code diffs for developers. V4 outputs reports, charts, and documents for business users who need deliverables they can share with stakeholders, not just code changes.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1218:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:852:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Without role separation, a single agent trying to refactor 20 files gets confused mid-stream, forgets earlier decisions, and loses the plot. Batch forces the "think once, execute many" discipline that humans use for migrations. The top agent holds the mental model; workers focus on one unit each. This is the exact pattern Runnable uses for its coordinator mode, ported to V4 as a skill. <b>Critical rule</b>: if you find yourself writing code in the coordinator, stop Ã¢â‚¬â€ that's the coordinator leaking into worker territory. Hand it to a <code>task</code> call instead.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1219:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:903:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:10px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> These aren't cosmetic changes. Each gap represented a behavioral regression vs Runnable that only showed up under specific conditions. For example, Gap #4 only surfaces during long sessions with many small tasks Ã¢â‚¬â€ exactly the scenario where users most need automatic quality gates. Together, these 7 fixes close the last measurable quality delta between V4 and Runnable on prompt engineering. <b>Remaining differences are infrastructure (ink TUI, remote sessions, voice), not agentic quality.</b></p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1221:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:970:      <p>Because the environment is different. Runnable is a CLI product built for Anthropic's own stack and a terminal-first workflow. V4 is optimized for SageMaker, notebooks, Bedrock pricing, and a user who often wants reports and visible widgets, not only terminal throughput.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1225:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:1062:      <tr><td>Is V4 fully identical to runnable?</td><td>~99%. Only remaining difference: Runnable has remote review sessions (ultrareview/CCR cloud infrastructure) Ã¢â‚¬â€ not applicable to SageMaker. See <code>compact_v4/docs/V4_6_RUNNABLE_UPGRADE.md</code>.</td></tr>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1226:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:1063:      <tr><td>Is V4 better than Runnable for SageMaker?</td><td>Yes. compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1418:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT_v4.html:105:  Why is <strong>Hermes</strong> (self-improving agent) different from <strong>Runnable / SageMaker V4</strong> (coding agent)?<br>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1422:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT_v4.html:245:    <strong>Key insight:</strong> Runnable and V4 are <em>task runners</em>. They do one job well, then stop. Hermes is a <em>persistent companion</em> that accumulates knowledge. Oracle needs to become the Hermes equivalent for sales Ã¢â‚¬â€ learning from each call and improving its next decision.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1423:.\compact_v5\docs\htmls\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT_v4.html:259:  <p class="desc">Left = what Runnable and SageMaker V4 do every session. Right = what Hermes does on top of that. The core loop is identical Ã¢â‚¬â€ the difference is what happens AFTER the response.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1472:.\compact_v5\_status\codex_reviews\phase-11.md:720:compact_v4\MAIN\agent\v3_architecture.html:215:    <p class="subtitle">A secure AI coding assistant that runs inside AWS SageMaker notebooks. It reads your code, writes files, edits Jupyter notebooks surgically, creates documents, generates charts, and helps you build software &mdash; all through a chat interface. v4.10.0 shipped 5 Runnable-parity upgrades. v4.10.1 added Context Collapse + Sonnet 4.5 default. v4.10.2 softened the verify-contract. v4.10.3 added ship-gate verifier + cache-boundary regression test + many-skill stress test + clearer permission denials. v4.10.4 closed the largest remaining gap: sub-agents now receive a bounded work-context handoff block (AGENT_STATUS slice + active todos + recent changed files) on top of v4.10.0's env-details. v4.10.5 adopted 3 Learning_Factory prompt-only patterns: post-compact resume protocol, structured summary sections, skill self-patching 4-rule check. v4.10.6 added the html skill: presentation / design / flowchart / architecture HTML deliverables, with screenshot-iteration loop. v4.10.7 hardened destructive-command coverage: ~50 new bash patterns + ~12 python patterns spanning cloud / IaC / orchestration / storage / DB CLI / persistence / system-overwrite, HIGH_RISK_TOOLS excluded from Always-Approve UI, cross-surface mirrored into Claude Code + Learning_Factory hooks. v4.10.8 closed the residual obfuscation escape route (base64/xxd/eval pipe-to-shell now blocked across more interpreters) and hard-blocked recursive folder removal from the agent (`rm -r/-rf/-fr/-R/--recursive`, `rmdir`, `Remove-Item -Recurse`, `shutil.rmtree`, `os.rmdir`, `os.removedirs`, `Path(...).rmdir()`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ single-file cleanup still allowed; the Ã°Å¸Â§Â¹ Clean button still works in-process. v4.10.9 closed Codex's third local-hook finding by adding a narrow backtick eval+downloader pattern (defense-in-depth only Ã¢â‚¬â€ eval was already excluded from the bash allowlist; zero new false-positive risk). v4.10.10 surfaced the previously-hardcoded `aws_bedrock_only` setting as a UI checkbox in cell 2 of `chat.ipynb` (default ticked = strict). Codex review on v4.10.10: PASS. 134/134 destructive-coverage tests + 122 v4 unit tests + 16/16 hook obfuscation self-test all green.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:1473:.\compact_v5\_status\codex_reviews\phase-11.md:721:compact_v4\MAIN\agent\v3_architecture.html:283:                <div class="card-desc">User asked: <em>"when bedrock only, then just it only Ã¢â‚¬â€ where to set it, in UI to control?"</em>. The previously-hardcoded <code>aws_bedrock_only</code> setting is now surfaced as a checkbox in cell 2 of <code>chat.ipynb</code>. <strong>Default ticked (strict, Bedrock-only).</strong> Untick before running cell 3 to allow S3 read/write, Textract, Lambda invoke, DynamoDB writes Ã¢â‚¬â€ all gated by the approval prompt; destructive AWS operations (delete bucket / object / table / function, terminate instances, etc.) remain hard-blocked at the per-method denylist and admin services blocked at boto3.client level. Cell 3 banner now displays AWS scope at startup. In-place refinements after comprehensive Codex re-review: inline <code>(?i)</code> flag on PowerShell <code>Remove-Item</code> regex (belt-and-suspenders Ã¢â‚¬â€ <code>re.IGNORECASE</code> already applied at matching layer line 1884) + clearer cell-by-cell setup text in cell 0. <strong>Codex re-review: PASS</strong> ("no findings, Remove-Item bypass closed, cell 0 setup text unambiguous, no additional bugs"). 134 destructive-coverage + 122 unit tests still green.</div>

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2349:.\compact_v5\_phase_2\v4_reference\chat.ipynb:6:   "source": "# SageAgent V4.10.10\n\n**Setup:** Run cells 1-3 in order:\n- **Cell 1** installs packages (run once per kernel).\n- **Cell 2** shows config widgets Ã¢â‚¬â€ set Model, Temperature, Workspace, Mock-Mode, and the **Bedrock-only** checkbox (default ticked = strict; untick to allow S3 / Textract / Lambda / DynamoDB writes with approval).\n- **Cell 3** reads all widget values into `CONFIG` and launches the agent. The \"Configuration Applied\" banner displays the chosen AWS scope.\n\n**Core files:** `sagemaker_agent.py` (the entire agent) + this notebook + `memory.md` (auto-populated) + `AGENT_STATUS.md` (long-running handoff) + `skills/` (10 skills + `/reflexion`).\n\n**v4.9 highlights** (full notes in `changelogs/CHANGELOG_v4.9.X.md`):\n- v4.9.0 Ã¢â‚¬â€ `auto_trigger: false` honoured; word-boundary keyword match; SYSTEM_PROMPT critique-handling section\n- v4.9.1 Ã¢â‚¬â€ `/unskill <name>` + sticky deactivation\n- v4.9.2 Ã¢â‚¬â€ minimum-ship zip\n- v4.9.3 Ã¢â‚¬â€ prompt-injection scanner; CSO description validator; `/reflexion` skill\n- v4.9.4 Ã¢â‚¬â€ IterationBudget shared parent+sub-agents; ErrorClassifier + jittered backoff; pre-compact tool-result pruning; opt-in auxiliary-model compaction (Haiku); structured Resolved/Pending Questions in summary\n- v4.9.5 Ã¢â‚¬â€ opt-in self-patching skills (agent proposes SKILL.md improvements, you review via `/skill suggestions` then `/skill apply <name>`)\n- v4.9.6 Ã¢â‚¬â€ production hardening: no default skill auto-load, Bedrock-safe compact history, fixed build worktree isolation, dirty-state worktree overlay, serialized parallel builds, durable status doc, local-git-only SageMaker policy\n- v4.9.7 Ã¢â‚¬â€ `/context` diagnostic: context percentage, top tool-output sources, duplicate file reads, suggested action\n- v4.10.0 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Runnable parity (5 phases): notebook_edit (surgical .ipynb cell editor), skill listing token budget cap, per-sub-agent env-details, model-aware context window auto-derive, reactive compact on Bedrock CONTEXT_OVERFLOW. 5 Codex per-phase reviews, 9 correctness issues caught and fixed.\n- v4.10.1 Ã¢â‚¬â€ same-day follow-up: segment-level Context Collapse (collapses 3+ stale tool round-trips into one synthetic Bedrock-safe pair) + default model switched to Sonnet 4.5 (cache activates from 1024 tokens vs Haiku 4096).\n- v4.10.2 Ã¢â‚¬â€ verify-contract softened: now SUGGEST /verify after 3+ logic edits and wait for user confirm; strict mode opt-in via CONFIG.enforce_verify_contract=True (Codex caught the prompt contradiction)\n- v4.10.3 Ã¢â‚¬â€ production-readiness review apply: ship-gate verifier (verify_ship_zip.py), cache-boundary regression test (6 tests), many-skill stress test (100/1000 skills), clearer permission denials\n- v4.10.4 Ã¢â‚¬â€ sub-agent work-context handoff (bounded AGENT_STATUS slice + active todos + last 10 changed files), Codex follow-up review fix\n- v4.10.5 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Learning_Factory pattern adoption: post-compact resume protocol + structured summary sections (#12 Standing Constraints + #13 Critical Don't-Forget) + skill self-patching 4-rule check (Repeated + Non-trivial + Generalizable + Real-pitfall)\n- v4.10.6 Ã¢â‚¬â€ html skill: presentation / design / flowchart / architecture HTML deliverables. Ships 3 reference templates (tabbed_design, presentation_slides, flowchart_page). Screenshot-iteration loop for SageMaker (no Playwright).\n- v4.10.7 Ã¢â‚¬â€ destructive-command hardening: ~50 new bash DANGEROUS_PATTERNS + ~12 new python DANGEROUS_PYTHON patterns. HIGH_RISK_TOOLS = {bash, python_exec, task, web_fetch} excluded from Always-Approve UI. 107-case test coverage. Cross-surface mirrored into Claude Code + Learning_Factory hooks.\n- v4.10.8 Ã¢â‚¬â€ obfuscation hardening + recursive folder removal hard-block. Bash: base64-decode pipe to extended interpreter set, xxd/od/hexdump decode chains, recursive `rm`/`rmdir`/PowerShell `Remove-Item -Recurse`. Python: blanket `shutil.rmtree`/`os.rmdir`/`os.removedirs`/`Path.rmdir` block from `python_exec`. 129-case test coverage.\n- v4.10.9 Ã¢â‚¬â€ backtick eval+downloader parity. One narrow new pattern catching `eval` + backtick + remote-fetcher (`curl`/`wget`/`fetch`) or decoder (`base64`/`xxd`/`hexdump`). Defense-in-depth only Ã¢â‚¬â€ `eval` is already excluded from v4's bash allowlist, zero new false-positive risk. 134-case test coverage.\n- **v4.10.10** Ã¢â‚¬â€ `aws_bedrock_only` UI toggle. Cell 2 gains a \"Bedrock-only\" checkbox (default ticked = strict). Untick to allow S3 read/write, Textract, Lambda invoke, DynamoDB writes Ã¢â‚¬â€ all with approval; destructive AWS ops remain hard-blocked. Cell 3 banner displays current AWS scope. Inline `(?i)` flag on PowerShell `Remove-Item` regex (belt-and-suspenders Ã¢â‚¬â€ `re.IGNORECASE` is already applied at the matching layer; explicit flag survives future refactor). Codex comprehensive review: PASS. Real coding bugs found: 0.\n\n**Full docs:** See `USER_GUIDE.md` for complete documentation.\n"

Python: blanket `shutil.rmtree`/`os.rmdir`/`os.removedirs`/`Path.rmdir` block from `python_exec`. 129-case test coverage.\n- v4.10.9 Ã¢â‚¬â€ backtick eval+downloader parity. One narrow new pattern catching `eval` + backtick + remote-fetcher (`curl`/`wget`/`fetch`) or decoder (`base64`/`xxd`/`hexdump`). Defense-in-depth only Ã¢â‚¬â€ `eval` is already excluded from v4's bash allowlist, zero new false-positive risk. 134-case test coverage.\n- **v4.10.10** Ã¢â‚¬â€ `aws_bedrock_only` UI toggle. Cell 2 gains a \"Bedrock-only\" checkbox (default ticked = strict). Untick to allow S3 read/write, Textract, Lambda invoke, DynamoDB writes Ã¢â‚¬â€ all with approval; destructive AWS ops remain hard-blocked. Cell 3 banner displays current AWS scope. Inline `(?i)` flag on PowerShell `Remove-Item` regex (belt-and-suspenders Ã¢â‚¬â€ `re.IGNORECASE` is already applied at the matching layer; explicit flag survives future refactor). Codex comprehensive review: PASS. Real coding bugs found: 0.\n\n**Full docs:** See `USER_GUIDE.md` for complete documentation.\n"

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2870:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\tabbed_design.html:101:  Why is <strong>Hermes</strong> (self-improving agent) different from <strong>Runnable / SageMaker V4</strong> (coding agent)?<br>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2874:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\tabbed_design.html:241:    <strong>Key insight:</strong> Runnable and V4 are <em>task runners</em>. They do one job well, then stop. Hermes is a <em>persistent companion</em> that accumulates knowledge. Oracle needs to become the Hermes equivalent for sales Ã¢â‚¬â€ learning from each call and improving its next decision.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2875:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\tabbed_design.html:255:  <p class="desc">Left = what Runnable and SageMaker V4 do every session. Right = what Hermes does on top of that. The core loop is identical Ã¢â‚¬â€ the difference is what happens AFTER the response.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2917:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:741:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Runnable targets Anthropic's direct API. Bedrock has different validation rules, pricing, and caching behavior. V4 handles these differences natively so you don't hit surprise errors or pay more than necessary.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2921:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:759:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Runnable outputs code diffs for developers. V4 outputs reports, charts, and documents for business users who need deliverables they can share with stakeholders, not just code changes.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2927:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:852:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Without role separation, a single agent trying to refactor 20 files gets confused mid-stream, forgets earlier decisions, and loses the plot. Batch forces the "think once, execute many" discipline that humans use for migrations. The top agent holds the mental model; workers focus on one unit each. This is the exact pattern Runnable uses for its coordinator mode, ported to V4 as a skill. <b>Critical rule</b>: if you find yourself writing code in the coordinator, stop Ã¢â‚¬â€ that's the coordinator leaking into worker territory. Hand it to a <code>task</code> call instead.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2928:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:903:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:10px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> These aren't cosmetic changes. Each gap represented a behavioral regression vs Runnable that only showed up under specific conditions. For example, Gap #4 only surfaces during long sessions with many small tasks Ã¢â‚¬â€ exactly the scenario where users most need automatic quality gates. Together, these 7 fixes close the last measurable quality delta between V4 and Runnable on prompt engineering. <b>Remaining differences are infrastructure (ink TUI, remote sessions, voice), not agentic quality.</b></p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2930:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:970:      <p>Because the environment is different. Runnable is a CLI product built for Anthropic's own stack and a terminal-first workflow. V4 is optimized for SageMaker, notebooks, Bedrock pricing, and a user who often wants reports and visible widgets, not only terminal throughput.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2934:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:1062:      <tr><td>Is V4 fully identical to runnable?</td><td>~99%. All 7 gaps from deep audit closed: verification contract, USE WHEN guidance, FP filtering, auto-nudge, critical reminder, fork semantics, skill discovery auto-surfacing. Only remaining difference: Runnable has remote review sessions (ultrareview/CCR cloud infrastructure) Ã¢â‚¬â€ not applicable to SageMaker. See <code>compact_v4/docs/V4_6_RUNNABLE_UPGRADE.md</code>.</td></tr>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:2935:.\compact_v4\MAIN\agent\skills\html\references\flowchart_page.html:1063:      <tr><td>Is V4 better than Runnable for SageMaker?</td><td>Yes. V4.6.1 matches Runnable's review/verification quality AND has SageMaker-specific features: budget slider with soft-stop, cache savings display, diminishing returns detection, document generation (Word/Excel/PDF/charts), 16 security layers with AST validation, Bedrock-native prompt caching, and 9 specialized skills (including new simplify, security-review).</td></tr>

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:3172:.\SESSION_STATE.md:510:Also caught: `AGENT_STATUS.md` was wrongly gitignored at `compact_v4/MAIN/agent/AGENT_STATUS.md` since round-8. That path is the SHIP TEMPLATE (committed), not the runtime-populated user file (which lives at `<workspace>/AGENT_STATUS.md` per CONFIG.workspace, typically NOT in the source dir). Fixed:
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:3177:.\SESSION_STATE.md:533:1. **SYSTEM_PROMPT '# System' section** gained one explicit post-compact resume rule: do NOT ask user what to do, read [CONVERSATION SUMMARY] + restored TODOs + AGENT_STATUS.md + recently-read files block, continue from first unchecked task.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:3180:.\SESSION_STATE.md:590:After v4.10.3 + final cleanup, user's Codex follow-up review pointed out the most important remaining weak spot: **sub-agents only got env-details (cwd / git HEAD / depth) but NOT the work context (current goal, active todos, changed files)**. AGENT_STATUS.md was loaded only at top-level (`subagent_depth==0`) by `_load_project_status()`. If the parent forgot to brief them in the `task` tool's `prompt` argument, sub-agents flew blind on the larger goal.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:3186:.\SESSION_STATE.md:738:5. **#41a Reactive Compact on CONTEXT_OVERFLOW** Ã¢â‚¬â€ when Bedrock rejects with "prompt is too long" / "too many tokens" / "input is too long", agent runs microcompact (or placeholder-summary fallback if microcompact freed less than `MICROCOMPACT_MIN_SAVINGS` Ã¢â‚¬â€ deliberately NO additional LLM call), clears file-read state, sets `_cache_broken_by_compact` (both branches), and retries the same request once. Cap: 1 reactive recovery per `run()` call. Other error categories surface unchanged. Retry stop-check mirrors original token-billing parity. Mirrors spirit of Runnable `reactiveCompact`. **5/5 tests.**

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:3591:.\_archive\compact_v3\ship\chat.ipynb:30:   "source": "# ============================================================\n# LAUNCH AGENT WITH CONFIGURATION\n# ============================================================\n\nfrom sagemaker_agent import CONFIG, create_chat_ui\nfrom IPython.display import display, HTML\n\n# Security & cost settings (previously in agent_config.json)\nCONFIG.aws_bedrock_only = True          # Block ALL AWS services except Bedrock\nCONFIG.require_tool_approval = True     # Show Approve/Deny dialog before execution\nCONFIG.session_cost_limit = 1.0         # Max $1.00 per session (warns at 80%, stops at 100%)\n\n# Custom slash commands: /review, /explain, /test\nCONFIG.custom_commands = {\n    \"review\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Read $ARGUMENTS and perform a thorough code review using this checklist:\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 1. Security (CRITICAL)\\n\"\n            \"Check: hardcoded secrets, SQL injection, command injection, XSS, path traversal, input validation, auth checks, sensitive data in logs\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 2. Code Quality (HIGH)\\n\"\n            \"Check: functions <50 lines, nesting <4 levels, specific error handling, clear naming, no dead code, no debug statements, DRY, consistent style\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 3. Performance (MEDIUM)\\n\"\n            \"Check: N+1 queries, O(n^2) algorithms, missing caching, unnecessary allocations, lazy processing for large collections\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 4. Architecture (MEDIUM)\\n\"\n            \"Check: separation of concerns, follows existing patterns, no circular deps, config externalized\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 5. Testing (MEDIUM)\\n\"\n            \"Check: critical paths covered, edge cases handled, tests independent, error paths tested\\n\"\n            \"\\n## Output: Summary, Issues (CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW with file:line), Positive observations, Suggestions, Rating X/10\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Full code review with 5-category checklist (security, quality, performance, architecture, testing)\",\n        \"agent\": \"plan\"\n    },\n    \"explain\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Read $ARGUMENTS and explain thoroughly:\\n\"\n            \"1. PURPOSE: What does this code do? What problem does it solve?\\n\"\n            \"2. ARCHITECTURE: How is it structured? What are the main components/classes/functions?\\n\"\n            \"3. DATA FLOW: How does data move through the code? Trace a typical request/call.\\n\"\n            \"4. KEY DECISIONS: What design patterns are used? Why were they chosen?\\n\"\n            \"5. DEPENDENCIES: What does it depend on? What depends on it?\\n\"\n            \"6. EDGE CASES: What error handling exists? What could go wrong?\\n\"\n            \"Use concrete examples from the actual code. Reference specific line numbers.\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Deep code explanation (purpose, architecture, data flow, patterns, dependencies, edge cases)\"\n    },\n    \"test\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Read $ARGUMENTS and write comprehensive tests:\\n\"\n            \"1. Happy path: normal expected behavior\\n\"\n            \"2. Edge cases: empty input, None, boundaries, duplicates, max values\\n\"\n            \"3. Error cases: invalid input, missing data, permission errors, timeouts\\n\"\n            \"4. Integration: how components work together\\n\"\n            \"Use pytest style. Each test: Arrange, Act, Assert. Test names describe what is tested.\\n\"\n            \"Target: minimum 8 test functions with good coverage of all branches.\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Generate comprehensive test suite (happy path, edge cases, error cases, integration)\"\n    },\n    \"verify\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Run 6-phase verification on the current workspace:\\n\"\n            \"Phase 1 BUILD: run build/compile if applicable (pip install -e . / npm run build)\\n\"\n            \"Phase 2 TYPES: run type checker (mypy/pyright/tsc) if available\\n\"\n            \"Phase 3 LINT: run linter (ruff/flake8/eslint) if available\\n\"\n            \"Phase 4 TESTS: run test suite (pytest/npm test) with coverage if available\\n\"\n            \"Phase 5 SECURITY: grep for hardcoded secrets, .env files, debug statements\\n\"\n            \"Phase 6 DIFF: git diff to review all changes\\n\"\n            \"\\nOutput a VERIFICATION REPORT: each phase PASS/FAIL/SKIP, issues found, ready for PR: YES/NO\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"6-phase verification: build, types, lint, tests, security scan, diff review\"\n    },\n    \"standards\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Review $ARGUMENTS against these coding standards:\\n\"\n            \"NAMING: descriptive vars, verb-noun functions, PascalCase classes, UPPER_SNAKE constants\\n\"\n            \"FUNCTIONS: single responsibility, <50 lines, <4 params, <4 nesting, early returns\\n\"\n            \"ERRORS: specific exceptions (not bare except), no swallowed errors, user-friendly messages\\n\"\n            \"PRINCIPLES: KISS, DRY, YAGNI\\n\"\n            \"Fix any violations found. Show before/after for each fix.\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Apply coding standards (naming, functions, errors, KISS/DRY/YAGNI)\"\n    },\n}\n\n# Apply configuration from widgets above\nCONFIG.model_id = AVAILABLE_MODELS[model_dropdown.value]\nCONFIG.region = REGION  # Sydney\nCONFIG.workspace = workspace_input.value\nCONFIG.max_turns = max_turns_slider.value\nCONFIG.mock_mode = mock_toggle.value\nCONFIG.temperature = TEMPERATURE_OPTIONS[temperature_dropdown.value]\nCONFIG.thinking_enabled = thinking_checkbox.value\nCONFIG.thinking_budget = THINKING_BUDGET_OPTIONS[thinking_budget_dropdown.value]\n\n# Display current config\nthinking_str = f\"Thinking: On (budget: {CONFIG.thinking_budget})\" if CONFIG.thinking_enabled else \"Thinking: Off\"\ndisplay(HTML(f\"\"\"\n<div style=\"background:#1e3a1e;padding:10px;border-radius:5px;margin:10px 0;color:#d4d4d4;\">\n<b>Configuration Applied</b><br>\nModel: {model_dropdown.value} | Region: Sydney | Temp: {CONFIG.temperature}<br>\n{thinking_str} | Mock: {'Yes' if CONFIG.mock_mode else 'No'}\n</div>\n\"\"\"))\n\n# Launch the chat interface\ncreate_chat_ui()"

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:3817:.\_archive\compact_v3\reviews\WHY_NOT_10.md:7:| 1 | **No Docker container** | -0.7 | SageMaker notebooks don't support Docker | Think of Docker as a locked room where code runs. If bad code escapes V3's software sandbox, there's no physical wall stopping it. SageMaker doesn't give us the locked room Ã¢â‚¬â€ we can only use software fences. |

Your task is to EXPLORE and CREATE A PLAN, NOT execute.\n\n# Plan Mode Rules\n1. **READ-ONLY**: You can ONLY use these tools:\n   - read_file, glob, grep, list_dir (explore codebase)\n   - todo_write, todo_read (track what you're planning)\n\n2. **NO WRITES**: Do NOT use:\n   - write_file, edit_file, bash, python_exec, create_word, create_excel\n\n3. **OUTPUT**: Create a detailed plan in your response:\n   - What needs to be done (steps)\n   - Which files need to be modified\n   - What changes will be made\n   - Any risks or considerations\n\n4. **FORMAT**: End your response with a plan summary like:\n   ```\n   ## Implementation Plan\n   1. [Step 1]\n   2. [Step 2]\n   ...\n\n   ## Files to Modify\n   - file1.py: [changes]\n   - file2.py: [changes]\n\n   ## Ready to Execute?\n   Turn off Plan Mode and send \"execute plan\" to proceed.\n   ```\n\nRemember: EXPLORE and PLAN only. No modifications!\n\"\"\"\n\n# Simple Compactor class (inline for complete version)\nclass SimpleCompactor:\n    PRUNE_PROTECT_TOKENS = 40000\n    PRUNE_MIN_SAVINGS = 10000\n    \n    @classmethod\n    def estimate_tokens(cls, text):\n        return len(str(text)) // 4\n    \n    @classmethod\n    def create_summary_prompt(cls):\n        \"\"\"Create summary prompt using Claude Code's 9-section format.\"\"\"\n        return \"\"\"<analysis>\nFirst, analyze the conversation to identify: main goal, technical concepts, files touched, errors encountered, and current progress.\n</analysis>\n\nCreate a detailed summary following these EXACT sections:\n\n1. **Primary Request and Intent**: What did the user explicitly ask for? What is their underlying goal?\n\n2. **Key Technical Concepts**: Technologies, frameworks, libraries, patterns discussed or used.\n\n3. **Files and Code Sections**: For each important file:\n   - File path (absolute)\n   - WHY it's important\n   - What changes were made (if any)\n   - Key code snippets (if relevant)\n\n4. **Errors and Fixes**: For each error encountered:\n   - The error message\n   - How it was fixed\n   - Any user feedback on the fix\n\n5. **Problem Solving**: Problems solved during the session, and any ongoing issues still unresolved.\n\n6. **ALL User Messages**: List EVERY user message verbatim (this prevents intent drift):\n   - \"message 1 exact text\"\n   - \"message 2 exact text\"\n   - (continue for all messages)\n\n7. **Pending Tasks**: Tasks mentioned but not yet completed.\n\n8. **Current Work**: Precise current state including:\n   - What step we're on\n   - What was just completed\n   - Relevant code context\n\n9. **Next Step**: Only if directly in line with user's explicit request. Include direct quotes from user if applicable.\n\nFormat as a comprehensive summary that preserves all context needed to continue seamlessly.\"\"\"\n    \n    @classmethod\n    def prune_tool_outputs(cls, messages, max_tokens):\n        \"\"\"Prune old tool outputs, keep recent ones.\"\"\"\n        pruned = []\n        total_protected = 0\n        tokens_saved = 0\n        \n        for msg in reversed(messages):\n            if msg.get(\"role\") == \"user\" and isinstance(msg.get(\"content\"), list):\n                new_content = []\n                for item in msg[\"content\"]:\n                    if isinstance(item, dict) and item.get(\"type\") == \"tool_result\":\n                        result = item.get(\"content\", \"\")\n                        tokens = cls.estimate_tokens(result)\n                        if total_protected + tokens <= cls.PRUNE_PROTECT_TOKENS:\n                            total_protected += tokens\n                            new_content.append(item)\n                        else:\n                            tokens_saved += tokens\n                            new_content.append({\n                                \"type\": \"tool_result\",\n                                \"tool_use_id\": item.get(\"tool_use_id\"),\n                                \"content\": f\"[... {len(result)} chars pruned ...]\"\n                            })\n                    else:\n                        new_content.append(item)\n                pruned.insert(0, {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": new_content})\n            else:\n                pruned.insert(0, msg)\n        \n        return pruned, tokens_saved\n    \n    @classmethod\n    def compact(cls, messages, summary):\n        \"\"\"Keep summary + last 5 messages.\"\"\"\n        summary_msg = {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": f\"[CONVERSATION SUMMARY]\\n{summary}\\n[END SUMMARY]\"}\n        last_messages = messages[-5:] if len(messages) > 5 else messages\n        return [summary_msg] + last_messages\n\nCOMPACTOR = SimpleCompactor()\n\n# UI State for message storage (SageMaker UI fix)\nui_state = {\n    \"messages\": [],  # Store as tuples: (role, content, tool_name, timestamp)\n    \"dark_mode\": True,  # Default to dark mode for SageMaker\n}\n\n# UI Components - HTML widget instead of Output (fixes SageMaker drifting)\nchat_display = widgets.HTML(value='')\ninput_box = widgets.Textarea(\n    placeholder='Type your message here...',\n    layout=widgets.Layout(width='100%', height='80px')\n)\n\n# Session controls\nsession_name_input = widgets.Text(placeholder='Session name (optional)', layout=widgets.Layout(width='180px'))\nsession_dropdown = widgets.Dropdown(description='', options=[('New Session', None)], layout=widgets.Layout(width='200px'))\nload_button = widgets.Button(description='Load', button_style='info', icon='folder-open')\nnew_button = widgets.Button(description='New', button_style='success', icon='plus')\n\n# Action buttons\nsend_button = widgets.Button(description='Send', button_style='primary', icon='paper-plane')\nclear_button = widgets.Button(description='Clear', button_style='warning', icon='trash')\nsave_button = widgets.Button(description='Save', button_style='info', icon='save')\ncompact_button = widgets.Button(description='Compact', button_style='', icon='compress', tooltip='Compress context')\n\n# Status displays\nstatus_label = widgets.HTML(value='<b style=\"color:#4caf50;\">Ã¢â€”Â Ready</b>')\ntokens_label = widgets.HTML(value='<span style=\"color:#6a9955;font-size:12px;\">Tokens: In:0 Out:0 | Total:0 | Calls:0</span>')\n\n# Dark mode toggle\ndark_toggle = widgets.ToggleButton(\n    value=True,\n    description='Dark Mode',\n    icon='moon',\n    button_style='',\n    layout=widgets.Layout(width='120px')\n)\n\n# Plan Mode toggle (OpenCode-style)\nplan_mode_toggle = widgets.ToggleButton(\n    value=False,\n    description='Plan Mode',\n    icon='map',\n    button_style='',\n    tooltip='When ON: Agent only reads/explores, creates plan. When OFF: Normal execution.',\n    layout=widgets.Layout(width='120px')\n)\n\n# Auto-compact checkbox (ON by default)\nauto_compact_checkbox = widgets.Checkbox(\n    value=True,\n    description='Auto-Compact',\n    indent=False,\n    tooltip='Automatically compact when context exceeds 90%'\n)\n\n# Approval dialog (not used with auto-allow, but kept for compatibility)\napproval_output = widgets.Output()\napprove_button = widgets.Button(description='Approve', button_style='success')\ndeny_button = widgets.Button(description='Deny', button_style='danger')\napproval_box = widgets.VBox([approval_output, widgets.HBox([approve_button, deny_button])])\napproval_box.layout.display = 'none'\n\n# Tool icons\nTOOL_ICONS = {\n    'read_file': 'Ã°Å¸â€œâ€“', 'write_file': 'Ã°Å¸â€œÂ', 'edit_file': 'Ã¢Å“ÂÃ¯Â¸Â',\n    'glob': 'Ã°Å¸â€Â', 'grep': 'Ã°Å¸â€Å½', 'list_dir': 'Ã°Å¸â€œÂ',\n    'bash': 'Ã°Å¸â€™Â»', 'python_exec': 'Ã°Å¸ÂÂ',\n    'create_word': 'Ã°Å¸â€œâ€ž', 'create_excel': 'Ã°Å¸â€œÅ ', 'create_markdown': 'Ã°Å¸â€œâ€¹',\n    'view_image': 'Ã°Å¸â€“Â¼Ã¯Â¸Â', 'semantic_search': 'Ã°Å¸Â§Â ',\n    'todo_write': 'Ã¢Å“â€¦', 'todo_read': 'Ã°Å¸â€œâ€¹',\n}\n\ndef escape_html(text):\n    return str(text).replace('&', '&amp;').replace('<', '&lt;').replace('>', '&gt;')\n\ndef render_chat():\n    \"\"\"Render all messages into HTML widget with internal scroll (SageMaker fix).\"\"\"\n    dark = ui_state[\"dark_mode\"]\n    bg = '#1e1e1e' if dark else '#ffffff'\n    fg = '#e0e0e0' if dark else '#333333'\n    border = '#444' if dark else '#ccc'\n    \n    msgs_html = []\n    for role, content, tool_name, ts in ui_state[\"messages\"]:\n        c = escape_html(content).replace('\\\\n', '<br>')\n        \n        if role == 'user':\n            msgs_html.append(f'''<div style=\"padding:10px;margin:5px 0;border-left:3px solid #26c6da;\">\n                <b style=\"color:#26c6da;\">[{ts}] You:</b>\n                <div style=\"white-space:pre-wrap;margin:5px 0;color:{fg};\">{c}</div>\n            </div>''')\n        elif role == 'assistant':\n            msgs_html.append(f'''<div style=\"padding:10px;margin:5px 0;border-left:3px solid #42a5f5;\">\n                <b style=\"color:#42a5f5;\">[{ts}] Agent:</b>\n                <div style=\"white-space:pre-wrap;margin:5px 0;color:{fg};font-family:monospace;\">{c}</div>\n            </div>''')\n        elif role == 'tool':\n            icon = TOOL_ICONS.get(tool_name, 'Ã°Å¸â€Â§')\n            truncated = c[:2000] + '...' if len(content) > 2000 else c\n            tool_bg = '#3d3222' if dark else '#fff8e1'\n            tool_fg = '#f0d080' if dark else '#f57c00'\n            msgs_html.append(f'''<details style=\"background:{tool_bg};padding:8px;margin:3px 0;border-radius:5px;font-size:12px;\">\n                <summary style=\"cursor:pointer;color:{tool_fg};\"><b>{icon} {tool_name}</b></summary>\n           sys_bg = '#4d2222' if dark else '#ffebee'\n            sys_fg = '#ff8a80' if dark else '#c62828'\n            msgs_html.append(f'''<div style=\"background:{sys_bg};color:{sys_fg};padding:8px;margin:3px 0;border-radius:5px;font-size:12px;\">\n                Ã¢Å¡Â Ã¯Â¸Â {c}\n            </div>''')\n    \n    content = ''.join(msgs_html) if msgs_html else f'<p style=\"color:{fg};text-align:center;padding:20px;\"><i>Type a message below to start chatting.</i></p>'\n    \n    # KEY: Scrollable div INSIDE the HTML content with flex-direction:column-reverse for auto-scroll\n    chat_display.value = f'''<div style=\"\n        height: 500px;\n        max-height: 500px;\n        overflow-y: auto;\n        overflow-x: hidden;\n        border: 1px solid {border};\n        background: {bg};\n        display: flex;\n        flex-direction: column-reverse;\n    \"><div style=\"padding:10px;font-family:system-ui,-apple-system,sans-serif;\">\n        {content}\n    </div></div>'''\n\ndef add_message(role, content, tool_name=None):\n    \"\"\"Add message and re-render.\"\"\"\n    ts = datetime.now().strftime('%H:%M:%S')\n    ui_state[\"messages\"].append((role, content, tool_name, ts))\n    render_chat()\n\ndef update_tokens(usage):\n    \"\"\"Update token display.\"\"\"\n    tokens_label.value = f'<span style=\"color:#6a9955;font-size:12px;\">Tokens: In:{usage[\"total_input\"]:,} Out:{usage[\"total_output\"]:,} | Total:{usage[\"total_input\"]+usage[\"total_output\"]:,} | Calls:{usage[\"api_calls\"]}</span>'\n\ndef update_session_list():\n    \"\"\"Refresh session dropdown.\"\"\"\n    recent = options.append((f\"Ã°Å¸â€œÂ {title}\", s['id']))\n    session_dropdown.options = options\n\ndef on_dark_toggle(change):\n    \"\"\"Handle dark mode toggle.\"\"\"\n    ui_state[\"dark_mode\"] = change['new']\n    render_chat()\n\ndark_toggle.observe(on_dark_toggle, names='value')\n\ndef on_send(b):\n    \"\"\"Handle send button click.\"\"\"\n    global current_session, agent\n    \n    message = input_box.value.strip()\n    if not message:\n        return\n    \n    input_box.value = ''\n    add_message('user', message)\n    status_label.value = '<b style=\"color:#ff9800;\">Ã¢â€¹Â¯ Processing...</b>'\n    \n    # Create session if needed\n    if current_session is None:\n        session_title = session_name_input.value.strip() if session_name_input.value.strip() else f\"Chat: {message[:40]}\"\n        current_session = sessions.create(session_title)\n        session_name_input.value = ''\n    \n    # Create tool context\n    ctx = ToolContext(\n        working_dir=workspace,\n        session_id=current_session.id,\n        security_manager=security,\n        audit_logger=audit,\n    )\n    \n    # Build system prompt (with Plan Mode if enabled)\n    prompt_builder = PromptBuilder()\n    system_prompt = prompt_builder.build(\n        workspace_root=workspace,\n        project_instructions=project_config.get_instructions(),\n    )\n    \n    if plan_mode_toggle.value:\n        add_message('system', 'Ã°Å¸â€œâ€¹ PLAN MODE: Agent will explore and create a plan (no modifications)')\n        system_prompt = system_prompt + \"\\\\n\\\\n\" + PLAN_MODE_PROMPT\n    \n    # Create agent\n    agent = AgentLoop(\n        client=client,\n        registry=registry,\n        system_prompt=system_prompt,\n        context=ctx,\n        max_turns=config.max_turns,\n        doom_threshold=config.doom_loop_threshold,\n        on_text=lambda t: add_message('assistant', t),\n        on_tool_call=lambda n, i: None,  # Don't show calling status\n        on_tool_result=lambda n, r: add_message('tool', r, n),\n        on_tokens=update_tokens,\n    )\n    \n    try:\n        response = agent.run(message)\n        \n        # Save to session\n        sessions.add_message(current_session, 'user', message)\n        sessions.add_message(current_session, 'assistant', response)\n        \n        # Check context and auto-compact if needed\n        status = context_mgr.get_status(agent.get_messages())\n        pct = status['usage_percent'] * 100\n        \n        if auto_compact_checkbox.value and pct >= 90:\n            add_message('system', f'Ã°Å¸â€â€ž Auto-compact triggered (context at {pct:.0f}%)...')\n            try:\n                messages = agent.get_messages()\n                pruned_msgs, tokens_saved = COMPACTOR.prune_tool_outputs(messages, 200000)\n                # Use 9-section summary prompt (available via COMPACTOR.create_summary_prompt())\n                summary = \"Conversation summary: \" + \"; \".join(\n                    (m.get(\"content\", \"\")[:60] if isinstance(m.get(\"content\"), str) else \"tool use\")\n                    for m in messages[:3]\n                )\n                compacted = COMPACTOR.compact(pruned_msgs, summary)\n                # Note: AgentLoop may not support direct message replacement\n                add_message('system', f'Ã¢Å“â€¦ Auto-compacted. Reduced from {len(messages)} to {len(compacted)} messages.')\n            except Exception as e:\n                add_message('system', f'Auto-compact failed: {e}')\n        \n        # Update status\n        if pct >= 90:\n            status_label.value = f'<b style=\"color:#f44336;\">Ã¢â€”Â Ready ({pct:.0f}% context - HIGH!)</b>'\n        elif pct >= 75:\n            status_label.value = f'<b style=\"color:#ff9800;\">Ã¢â€”Â Ready ({pct:.0f}% context)</b>'\n        else:\n            status_label.value = f'<b style=\"color:#4caf50;\">Ã¢â€”Â Ready ({pct:.0f}% context)</b>'\n        \n        if plan_mode_toggle.value:\n            status_label.value = status_label.value.replace('Ready', 'Ã°Å¸â€œâ€¹ Plan Mode')\n        \n    except Exception as e:\n        add_message('system', f'Error: {str(e)}')\n        status_label.value = '<b style=\"color:#f44336;\">Ã¢â€”Â Error</b>'\n\ndef on_clear(b):\n    \"\"\"Clear chat and start new session.\"\"\"\n    global current_session, agent\n    current_session = None\n    agent = None\n    ui_state[\"messages\"] = []\n    tokens_label.value = '<span style=\"color:#6a9955;font-size:12px;\">Tokens: In:0 Out:0 | Total:0 | Calls:0</span>'\n    status_label.value = '<b style=\"color:#4caf50;\">Ã¢â€”Â Ready</b>'\n    render_chat()\n\ndef on_save(b):\n    \"\"\"Save current session.\"\"\"\n    if current_session:\n        sessions.save(current_session)\n        add_message('system', f'Session saved: {current_session.id}')\n        update_session_list()\n    else:\n        add_message('system', 'No session to save')\n\ndef on_load(b):\n    \"\"\"Load selected session.\"\"\"\n    global current_session, agent\n    session_id = session_dropdown.value\n    if not session_id:\n        on_clear(None)\n        return\n    \n    loaded = sessions.load(session_id)\n    if loaded:\n        current_session = loaded\n        ui_state[\"messages\"] = []\n        add_message('system', f'Loaded session: {loaded.title}')\n        update_session_list()\n    else:\n        add_message('system', f'Failed to load session: {session_id}')\n\ndef on_new(b):\n    \"\"\"Start new session.\"\"\"\n    global current_session, agent\n    current_session = None\n    agent = None\n    ui_state[\"messages\"] = []\n    session_dropdown.value = None\n    render_chat()\n    status_label.value = '<b style=\"color:#4caf50;\">Ã¢â€”Â Ready (New)</b>'\n\ndef on_compact(b):\n    \"\"\"Manually compact context.\"\"\"\n    if not agent:\n        add_message('system', 'No conversation to compact.')\n        return\n    \n    add_message('system', 'Compacting conversation...')\n    try:\n        messages = agent.get_messages()\n        pruned_msgs, tokens_saved = COMPACTOR.prune_tool_outputs(messages, 200000)\n        if tokens_saved > 0:\n            add_message('system', f'Stage 1: Pruned old tool outputs (~{tokens_saved:,} tokens saved)')\n        \n        # Use 9-section summary prompt (available via COMPACTOR.create_summary_prompt())\n        summary = \"Conversation summary: \" + \"; \".join(\n            (m.get(\"content\", \"\")[:60] if isinstance(m.get(\"content\"), str) else \"tool use\")\n            for m in messages[:3]\n        )\n        compacted = COMPACTOR.compact(pruned_msgs, summary)\n        add_message('system', f'Compacted: {len(messages)} Ã¢â€ â€™ {len(compacted)} messages. Click Save to persist.')\n    except Exception as e:\n        add_message('system', f'Compact failed: {e}')\n\nsend_button.on_click(on_send)\nclear_button.on_click(on_clear)\nsave_button.on_click(on_save)\ncompact_button.on_click(on_compact)\nload_button.on_click(on_load)\nnew_button.on_click(on_new)\n\n# Layout - Row 1: [Name] [Session Ã¢â€“Â¼] [Ã°Å¸â€œÂLoad] [+New] | [Model Ã¢â€“Â¼]\nheader = widgets.HTML('<h2 style=\"color:#569cd6;margin:0 0 10px 0;\">SageMaker Coding Agent v2.0.0</h2>')\nrow1 = widgets.HBox([\n    session_name_input, session_dropdown, load_button, new_button,\n    widgets.HTML('<span style=\"margin:0 10px;\">|</span>'),\n    dark_toggle\n])\nrow2 = widgets.HBox([plan_mode_toggle, auto_compact_checkbox])\nbutton_row = widgets.HBox([send_button, clear_button, save_button, compact_button, status_label])\ntokens_row = widgets.HBox([tokens_label])\nchat_box = widgets.VBox([header, row1, row2, chat_display, input_box, button_row, tokens_row])\n\n# Initial render\nupdate_session_list()\nrender_chat()\n\ndisplay(chat_box)"
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4428:.\_archive\compact_v3\MAIN\agent\chat.ipynb:30:   "source": "# ============================================================\n# LAUNCH AGENT WITH CONFIGURATION\n# ============================================================\n\nfrom sagemaker_agent import CONFIG, create_chat_ui\nfrom IPython.display import display, HTML\n\n# Security & cost settings (previously in agent_config.json)\nCONFIG.aws_bedrock_only = True          # Block ALL AWS services except Bedrock\nCONFIG.require_tool_approval = True     # Show Approve/Deny dialog before execution\nCONFIG.session_cost_limit = 1.0         # Max $1.00 per session (warns at 80%, stops at 100%)\n\n# Custom slash commands: /review, /explain, /test\nCONFIG.custom_commands = {\n    \"review\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Read $ARGUMENTS and perform a thorough code review using this checklist:\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 1. Security (CRITICAL)\\n\"\n            \"Check: hardcoded secrets, SQL injection, command injection, XSS, path traversal, input validation, auth checks, sensitive data in logs\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 2. Code Quality (HIGH)\\n\"\n            \"Check: functions <50 lines, nesting <4 levels, specific error handling, clear naming, no dead code, no debug statements, DRY, consistent style\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 3. Performance (MEDIUM)\\n\"\n            \"Check: N+1 queries, O(n^2) algorithms, missing caching, unnecessary allocations, lazy processing for large collections\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 4. Architecture (MEDIUM)\\n\"\n            \"Check: separation of concerns, follows existing patterns, no circular deps, config externalized\\n\"\n            \"\\n## 5. Testing (MEDIUM)\\n\"\n            \"Check: critical paths covered, edge cases handled, tests independent, error paths tested\\n\"\n            \"\\n## Output: Summary, Issues (CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW with file:line), Positive observations, Suggestions, Rating X/10\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Full code review with 5-category checklist (security, quality, performance, architecture, testing)\",\n        \"agent\": \"plan\"\n    },\n    \"explain\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Read $ARGUMENTS and explain thoroughly:\\n\"\n            \"1. PURPOSE: What does this code do? What problem does it solve?\\n\"\n            \"2. ARCHITECTURE: How is it structured? What are the main components/classes/functions?\\n\"\n            \"3. DATA FLOW: How does data move through the code? Trace a typical request/call.\\n\"\n            \"4. KEY DECISIONS: What design patterns are used? Why were they chosen?\\n\"\n            \"5. DEPENDENCIES: What does it depend on? What depends on it?\\n\"\n            \"6. EDGE CASES: What error handling exists? What could go wrong?\\n\"\n            \"Use concrete examples from the actual code. Reference specific line numbers.\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Deep code explanation (purpose, architecture, data flow, patterns, dependencies, edge cases)\"\n    },\n    \"test\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Read $ARGUMENTS and write comprehensive tests:\\n\"\n            \"1. Happy path: normal expected behavior\\n\"\n            \"2. Edge cases: empty input, None, boundaries, duplicates, max values\\n\"\n            \"3. Error cases: invalid input, missing data, permission errors, timeouts\\n\"\n            \"4. Integration: how components work together\\n\"\n            \"Use pytest style. Each test: Arrange, Act, Assert. Test names describe what is tested.\\n\"\n            \"Target: minimum 8 test functions with good coverage of all branches.\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Generate comprehensive test suite (happy path, edge cases, error cases, integration)\"\n    },\n    \"verify\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Run 6-phase verification on the current workspace:\\n\"\n            \"Phase 1 BUILD: run build/compile if applicable (pip install -e . / npm run build)\\n\"\n            \"Phase 2 TYPES: run type checker (mypy/pyright/tsc) if available\\n\"\n            \"Phase 3 LINT: run linter (ruff/flake8/eslint) if available\\n\"\n            \"Phase 4 TESTS: run test suite (pytest/npm test) with coverage if available\\n\"\n            \"Phase 5 SECURITY: grep for hardcoded secrets, .env files, debug statements\\n\"\n            \"Phase 6 DIFF: git diff to review all changes\\n\"\n            \"\\nOutput a VERIFICATION REPORT: each phase PASS/FAIL/SKIP, issues found, ready for PR: YES/NO\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"6-phase verification: build, types, lint, tests, security scan, diff review\"\n    },\n    \"standards\": {\n        \"template\": (\n            \"Review $ARGUMENTS against these coding standards:\\n\"\n            \"NAMING: descriptive vars, verb-noun functions, PascalCase classes, UPPER_SNAKE constants\\n\"\n            \"FUNCTIONS: single responsibility, <50 lines, <4 params, <4 nesting, early returns\\n\"\n            \"ERRORS: specific exceptions (not bare except), no swallowed errors, user-friendly messages\\n\"\n            \"PRINCIPLES: KISS, DRY, YAGNI\\n\"\n            \"Fix any violations found. Show before/after for each fix.\"\n        ),\n        \"description\": \"Apply coding standards (naming, functions, errors, KISS/DRY/YAGNI)\"\n    },\n}\n\n# Apply configuration from widgets above\nCONFIG.model_id = AVAILABLE_MODELS[model_dropdown.value]\nCONFIG.region = REGION  # Sydney\nCONFIG.workspace = workspace_input.value\nCONFIG.max_turns = max_turns_slider.value\nCONFIG.mock_mode = mock_toggle.value\nCONFIG.temperature = TEMPERATURE_OPTIONS[temperature_dropdown.value]\nCONFIG.thinking_enabled = thinking_checkbox.value\nCONFIG.thinking_budget = THINKING_BUDGET_OPTIONS[thinking_budget_dropdown.value]\n\n# Display current config\nthinking_str = f\"Thinking: On (budget: {CONFIG.thinking_budget})\" if CONFIG.thinking_enabled else \"Thinking: Off\"\ndisplay(HTML(f\"\"\"\n<div style=\"background:#1e3a1e;padding:10px;border-radius:5px;margin:10px 0;color:#d4d4d4;\">\n<b>Configuration Applied</b><br>\nModel: {model_dropdown.value} | Region: Sydney | Temp: {CONFIG.temperature}<br>\n{thinking_str} | Mock: {'Yes' if CONFIG.mock_mode else 'No'}\n</div>\n\"\"\"))\n\n# Launch the chat interface\ncreate_chat_ui()"

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4708:.\Documentations\V4_VS_RUNNABLE_ARCHITECTURE.md:119:**V4 is sufficient.** You're a single user in a notebook Ã¢â‚¬â€ you don't need OTEL pipelines, Datadog dashboards, or glob-pattern permission rules. V4's approval dialog + cost display + audit log covers your needs. Runnable's monitoring is for teams running Claude Code at scale with CI/CD integrations.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4718:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\EVALUATION_V4_vs_RUNNABLE_vs_CLAW.md:18:**Bottom line**: V4 is a real agent that does real work. Claw-Code is a JSON catalog pretending to be an agent. Runnable is Claude Code itself. PDF is a specialized pipeline proving hybrid deterministic+LLM beats pure LLM. They're not comparable products Ã¢â‚¬â€ they're different categories.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4719:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\EVALUATION_V4_vs_RUNNABLE_vs_CLAW.md:56:6. **Pre-Edit Staleness Check** Ã¢â‚¬â€ Tracks file mtime, aborts edit if file changed since last read (0.5s tolerance). Prevents silent overwrites. Runnable doesn't have this.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4725:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT.html:89:  Why is <strong>Hermes</strong> (self-improving agent) different from <strong>Runnable / SageMaker V4</strong> (coding agent)?<br>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4729:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT.html:229:    <strong>Key insight:</strong> Runnable and V4 are <em>task runners</em>. They do one job well, then stop. Hermes is a <em>persistent companion</em> that accumulates knowledge. Oracle needs to become the Hermes equivalent for sales Ã¢â‚¬â€ learning from each call and improving its next decision.
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4730:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\HERMES_VS_CODING_AGENT.html:243:  <p class="desc">Left = what Runnable and SageMaker V4 do every session. Right = what Hermes does on top of that. The core loop is identical Ã¢â‚¬â€ the difference is what happens AFTER the response.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4814:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:741:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Runnable targets Anthropic's direct API. Bedrock has different validation rules, pricing, and caching behavior. V4 handles these differences natively so you don't hit surprise errors or pay more than necessary.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4818:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:759:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Runnable outputs code diffs for developers. V4 outputs reports, charts, and documents for business users who need deliverables they can share with stakeholders, not just code changes.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4824:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:852:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:8px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> Without role separation, a single agent trying to refactor 20 files gets confused mid-stream, forgets earlier decisions, and loses the plot. Batch forces the "think once, execute many" discipline that humans use for migrations. The top agent holds the mental model; workers focus on one unit each. This is the exact pattern Runnable uses for its coordinator mode, ported to V4 as a skill. <b>Critical rule</b>: if you find yourself writing code in the coordinator, stop Ã¢â‚¬â€ that's the coordinator leaking into worker territory. Hand it to a <code>task</code> call instead.</p>
compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4825:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\PS_FLOWCHART_V4.html:903:        <p style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px;margin-top:10px;"><b>Why this matters:</b> These aren't cosmetic changes. Each gap represented a behavioral regression vs Runnable that only showed up under specific conditions. For example, Gap #4 only surfaces during long sessions with many small tasks Ã¢â‚¬â€ exactly the scenario where users most need automatic quality gates. Together, these 7 fixes close the last measurable quality delta between V4 and Runnable on prompt engineering. <b>Remaining differences are infrastructure (ink TUI, remote sessions, voice), not agentic quality.</b></p>

compact_v5/_phase_2\codex_review\CODEX_REVIEW_v2.md:4858:.\PS_ClaudeCode_Insights\PS_RUNNABLE_VS_LANGGRAPH.html:826:<b>What is enforcement?</b> Enforcement = stopping the AI from doing something dangerous. This is NOT the same as asking the AI nicely ("please don't delete files"). Enforcement means the system STRUCTURALLY PREVENTS the action. The AI can want to <code>rm -rf /</code> all it wants Ã¢â‚¬â€ the enforcement layer makes it impossible. This is the most important lesson from Claude Code.

compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\chat.ipynb:6:   "source": "# SageAgent V4.10.10\n\n**Setup:** Run cells 1-3 in order:\n- **Cell 1** installs packages (run once per kernel).\n- **Cell 2** shows config widgets â€” set Model, Temperature, Workspace, Mock-Mode, and the **Bedrock-only** checkbox (default ticked = strict; untick to allow S3 / Textract / Lambda / DynamoDB writes with approval).\n- **Cell 3** reads all widget values into `CONFIG` and launches the agent. The \"Configuration Applied\" banner displays the chosen AWS scope.\n\n**Core files:** `sagemaker_agent.py` (the entire agent) + this notebook + `memory.md` (auto-populated) + `AGENT_STATUS.md` (long-running handoff) + `skills/` (10 skills + `/reflexion`).\n\n**v4.9 highlights** (full notes in `changelogs/CHANGELOG_v4.9.X.md`):\n- v4.9.0 â€” `auto_trigger: false` honoured; word-boundary keyword match; SYSTEM_PROMPT critique-handling section\n- v4.9.1 â€” `/unskill <name>` + sticky deactivation\n- v4.9.2 â€” minimum-ship zip\n- v4.9.3 â€” prompt-injection scanner; CSO description validator; `/reflexion` skill\n- v4.9.4 â€” IterationBudget shared parent+sub-agents; ErrorClassifier + jittered backoff; pre-compact tool-result pruning; opt-in auxiliary-model compaction (Haiku); structured Resolved/Pending Questions in summary\n- v4.9.5 â€” opt-in self-patching skills (agent proposes SKILL.md improvements, you review via `/skill suggestions` then `/skill apply <name>`)\n- v4.9.6 â€” production hardening: no default skill auto-load, Bedrock-safe compact history, fixed build worktree isolation, dirty-state worktree overlay, serialized parallel builds, durable status doc, local-git-only SageMaker policy\n- v4.9.7 â€” `/context` diagnostic: context percentage, top tool-output sources, duplicate file reads, suggested action\n- v4.10.0 â€” Runnable parity (5 phases): notebook_edit (surgical .ipynb cell editor), skill listing token budget cap, per-sub-agent env-details, model-aware context window auto-derive, reactive compact on Bedrock CONTEXT_OVERFLOW. 5 Codex per-phase reviews, 9 correctness issues caught and fixed.\n- v4.10.1 â€” same-day follow-up: segment-level Context Collapse (collapses 3+ stale tool round-trips into one synthetic Bedrock-safe pair) + default model switched to Sonnet 4.5 (cache activates from 1024 tokens vs Haiku 4096).\n- v4.10.2 â€” verify-contract softened: now SUGGEST /verify after 3+ logic edits and wait for user confirm; strict mode opt-in via CONFIG.enforce_verify_contract=True (Codex caught the prompt contradiction)\n- v4.10.3 â€” production-readiness review apply: ship-gate verifier (verify_ship_zip.py), cache-boundary regression test (6 tests), many-skill stress test (100/1000 skills), clearer permission denials\n- v4.10.4 â€” sub-agent work-context handoff (bounded AGENT_STATUS slice + active todos + last 10 changed files), Codex follow-up review fix\n- v4.10.5 â€” Learning_Factory pattern adoption: post-compact resume protocol + structured summary sections (#12 Standing Constraints + #13 Critical Don't-Forget) + skill self-patching 4-rule check (Repeated + Non-trivial + Generalizable + Real-pitfall)\n- v4.10.6 â€” html skill: presentation / design / flowchart / architecture HTML deliverables. Ships 3 reference templates (tabbed_design, presentation_slides, flowchart_page). Screenshot-iteration loop for SageMaker (no Playwright).\n- v4.10.7 â€” destructive-command hardening: ~50 new bash DANGEROUS_PATTERNS + ~12 new python DANGEROUS_PYTHON patterns. HIGH_RISK_TOOLS = {bash, python_exec, task, web_fetch} excluded from Always-Approve UI. 107-case test coverage. Cross-surface mirrored into Claude Code + Learning_Factory hooks.\n- v4.10.8 â€” obfuscation hardening + recursive folder removal hard-block. Bash: base64-decode pipe to extended interpreter set, xxd/od/hexdump decode chains, recursive `rm`/`rmdir`/PowerShell `Remove-Item -Recurse`. Python: blanket `shutil.rmtree`/`os.rmdir`/`os.removedirs`/`Path.rmdir` block from `python_exec`. 129-case test coverage.\n- v4.10.9 â€” backtick eval+downloader parity. One narrow new pattern catching `eval` + backtick + remote-fetcher (`curl`/`wget`/`fetch`) or decoder (`base64`/`xxd`/`hexdump`). Defense-in-depth only â€” `eval` is already excluded from v4's bash allowlist, zero new false-positive risk. 134-case test coverage.\n- **v4.10.10** â€” `aws_bedrock_only` UI toggle. Cell 2 gains a \"Bedrock-only\" checkbox (default ticked = strict). Untick to allow S3 read/write, Textract, Lambda invoke, DynamoDB writes â€” all with approval; destructive AWS ops remain hard-blocked. Cell 3 banner displays current AWS scope. Inline `(?i)` flag on PowerShell `Remove-Item` regex (belt-and-suspenders â€” `re.IGNORECASE` is already applied at the matching layer; explicit flag survives future refactor). Codex comprehensive review: PASS. Real coding bugs found: 0.\n\n**Full docs:** See `USER_GUIDE.md` for complete documentation.\n"

compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\chat.md:90:**v4.10.0 â€” Runnable parity (5 additions, Codex-reviewed per phase):**
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\chat.md:91:1. **`notebook_edit` tool** â€” surgically insert / replace / delete a single cell in an existing `.ipynb` (atomic write, preserves cell IDs, resets execution state on code cells). Use this â€” NOT `create_notebook` â€” when modifying an existing notebook so you don't blow away cells you didn't touch.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\chat.md:103:2. **Runnable comparison close-out** â€” keeps V4 focused on SageMaker self-use: local git tree, explicit skills, durable status doc, and no GitHub/remote runtime assumption.

compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:94:- **Post-compact resume protocol** â€” system prompt now tells the agent: after compact, do NOT ask "what would you like me to do?". Read the `[CONVERSATION SUMMARY]` block + restored TODOs + AGENT_STATUS.md + recently-read files block, and continue from the first unchecked task. Asks user only when blocked on a real decision.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:98:Deliberately NOT adopted: LF's full hook ecosystem (not a SageMaker fit), smart approval LLM judge (cost), tool-failure 5/3/8 thresholds (current 3-repeat doom-loop is already stricter â€” NOT loosened), heavy rollback ecosystem (local git is enough).
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:104:User's Codex follow-up review pointed out that v4.10.3 sub-agents only saw env-details (cwd / git HEAD / depth) but NOT the work context (current goal, active todos, changed files). If the parent forgot to brief them in the prompt, sub-agents flew blind. Fixed:
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:109:  - Last 10 changed-file paths (NO diff bodies â€” would blow the budget)
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:134:- **`#41b` Context Collapse (segment-level)** â€” after microcompact replaces stale tool outputs with markers, runs of 3+ consecutive stale tool round-trips collapse into one synthetic 2-message pair. Strict classification: thinking / image / unknown block types in the assistant message block the collapse; marker match is exact equality, not substring. Mirrors Runnable's `contextCollapse` feature gate. 12 tests, Codex PASS.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:139:Five Runnable-parity upgrades. Each one has its own regression test file and was reviewed by Codex (gpt-5.3-codex) per phase.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:183:**It is NOT a terminal app.** Everything runs as Python code inside the Jupyter kernel. The chat UI is built with `ipywidgets` (text box + send button + output area). You never need to use a terminal â€” though the agent can run shell commands internally via its `bash` tool.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:282:This app does **NOT** open a terminal or command line. Everything runs as **Python code inside the Jupyter notebook kernel**. When you open `chat.ipynb` and run the cells, Python code executes in the background â€” creating the chat UI, handling your messages, calling the AWS API, and executing tools.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:442:| `notebook_edit` | **V4.10.0:** Surgically edit ONE cell of an EXISTING `.ipynb` (insert/replace/delete). Atomic write, preserves cell IDs. Use this â€” NOT `create_notebook` â€” when modifying an existing notebook so you don't blow away cells you didn't touch. | "Add a new code cell after cell 3 that loads data" |
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:528:| `/checkpoint restore <name>` | Restore todos from checkpoint (files NOT auto-reverted â€” review list then `/revert` per file) | `/checkpoint restore phase-1-complete` |
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:548:**Note on `/regression`:** Thin wrapper â€” prints git diff stat, session edit counts, and a suggested test command. Does NOT run tests itself (you run them via bash) and does NOT track baselines. For automated adversarial testing use `/verify`. For a full ship gate use `/done`.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:554:V4 spawns sub-agents through the **`task` tool**. There is **no agent-team / inter-process coordination** (that's a Runnable feature for multi-Claude scenarios â€” irrelevant for SageMaker self-use). What you have is one parent agent that can spawn typed children. **7 agent types**, each with its own tool allowlist and prompt suffix:
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:575:- Per-agent token breakdown is NOT displayed; only the running total.
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:583:NOT this:
compact_v5/_phase_2\v4_reference\USER_GUIDE.md:611:Skills require explicit activation via `/command`. They do NOT auto-trigger on keywords by default â€” this prevents unwanted skill activation when you're just having a conversation. (This was a real bug in V4.8: a message saying "review the X code" would silently inject a 8K-char `clara-review` audit methodology into every prompt thereafter. V4.9.6 closed it with two independent gates.)

compact_v5/_phase_2\wave_2\runnable_services_api\REPORT.md:5:Runnable error handling (1207 LOC), retry logic (823 LOC), and cache-break detection (728 LOC) are substantially more sophisticated than v5 baseline. Runnable tracks 50+ error categories vs v5 8. Retry features 529-gating, persistent unattended mode, fast-mode fallback, max-tokens adjustment. Cache-break detection uses per-tool hashing, beta tracking, effort/overage latching. Most MISSING from v5 but architecturally clean.
